, then we can project back down to conclude that the conjecture holds for
X
. Therefore, it suffices to show that, when
f
is non-exceptional,
\overline{X}
is the product of a special subvariety of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},\mathbb{F}}
with a subvariety of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},\mathbb{F}}
.

From here, we can further reduce to the case where
\bm{A}
is normalized (Lemma
8.1
) and simple. Suppose that the conjecture holds when
\bm{A}
is simple. Then for general
{\bm{A}}
, there is a nonempty subset
\mathbf{J}\subseteq\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}
, and a Zariski dense sub-collection
\bm{B}\subseteq\bm{A}
, such that
\mathbf{I}_{\bm{B}}=\mathbf{J}
and
\bm{B}
is simple. By assumption,
\overline{X}
is the product of a special subvariety of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{B}},\mathbb{F}}
with a subvariety
Y\subseteq\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{B}},\mathbb{F}}
. If
\mathbf{I}_{\bm{B}}=\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}
, then we are done. Otherwise, project all special subvarieties in
\bm{A}
down to
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{B}},\mathbb{F}}
and repeat the same process for
Y
.

Therefore, in the following we can assume
f
is non-exceptional and
\bm{A}
is normalized and simple.
Let
X^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow X_{0}
be an étale open subset satisfying Proposition
8.3
and let
\mathscr{F}
be the lisse subsheaf thus arise. Pick
x\in X^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{F})
and, by abuse of notation, denote by
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbb{F}}
the composition of the étale open subset
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
and the immersion
X\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbb{F}}
, and let
\mathscr{T}_{f,x}
and
T_{f,x}
be objects defined in §
6.1
. We then have

(8.3.2)

\mathscr{F}_{x}\subseteq\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},x},\text{ and }\mathscr{F}_{x}\otimes\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\wedge}\subseteq X^{\prime/x}\subseteq\mathscr{T}_{f,x}.

Recall that for
\mathbf{J}\subseteq\mathbf{I}
,
f_{\mathbf{J}}
is the composition of
f
with the projection
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbb{F}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{J},\mathbb{F}}
. Theorem
7.11
implies that there is a special subvariety
\mathcal{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}}
such that
\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}^{/x}=\mathscr{T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}
.
Let
Y
be the Zariski closure of the projections of the elements in
\bm{A}
to
\mathscr{S}_{f_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F}}
, and let
\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}^{+}
be the unique irreducible component of
\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}
passing through
x
. Then

(8.3.3)

\overline{X}\subseteq\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}^{+}\times Y.

By definition, the projection
\overline{X}\rightarrow Y
is surjective. It suffices to show that this is actually an equality.

Claim
.

We have
T_{f,x}={T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}\oplus{T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},x}}
and
T_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}=\mathscr{F}_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

For a subset
\mathbf{J}\subseteq\mathbf{I}
, let
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{J}}
be the projection of
\mathscr{F}
to
\mathscr{E}_{\mathbf{J}}
. Then
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{J},x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\subseteq T_{f_{\mathbf{J}},x}
and
\mathscr{F}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}=0
. It is then a situation to apply the monodromy argument. From the discussion made in §
8.2.3
, we see that
\mathscr{F}_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a subrepresentation of
G(\gr\mathbb{M}_{p,f},x)\curvearrowright T_{f,x}
, hence each
\mathscr{F}_{i,x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a subrepresentation of
G(\gr\mathbb{M}_{p,f_{i}},x)\curvearrowright T_{f_{i},x}
. This implies that
\mathscr{F}_{i,x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}=T_{f_{i},x}
for
i\in\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}
(if
\dim T_{f_{i},x}\neq 2
, this follows from the proof of Theorem
8.4
; if
\dim T_{f_{i},x}=2
, this follows from Remark
8.5
and our assumption that
f
is non-exceptional). Now by Lemma
7.9
, there is a partition
\mathbf{I}=\prod_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\mathbf{I}^{k}
, with the property that for each
k
there is an iso-graph
G^{k}
(
resp
.
T^{k}_{x}
) over
\mathbf{I}^{k}
, such that
G(\mathbb{M}_{p,f},x)^{\ad}=\prod_{k\in\mathcal{K}}G^{k}
(
resp
.
T_{f,x}=\prod_{k\in\mathcal{K}}T^{k}_{x}
). We must have
T_{f,x}={T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}\oplus{T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},x}}
, for otherwise Lemma
8.6
(
1
) would imply the existence of an index
j\in\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}
with
\mathscr{F}_{j,x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\neq 0
. On the other hand,
\mathscr{F}_{i,x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a subrep of
\theta:G(\gr_{\neq 0}\mathbb{M}_{p,f},x)\curvearrowright T_{f,x}
, which is a torus action. Since
G(\gr_{\neq 0}\mathbb{M}_{p,f},x)
goes isomorphically onto its image in
G(\mathbb{M}_{p,f},x)^{\ad}
, we can split
\theta
as a direct sum indexed by
\mathcal{K}
, such that each summand is a torus scaling. We then apply Lemma
8.6
(
2
) to conclude that
T_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}=\mathscr{F}_{x,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

Let
y
be the projection of
x
to
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},\mathbb{F}}
. The
Claim
immediately shows that the fiber
{X}_{y}
has dimension equal to
\dim T_{f_{\mathbb{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}
, hence

(8.3.4)

\overline{X}_{y}=\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}^{+}\times\{y\}.

Let
x^{\prime}
be another point of
X^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{F})
. The same argument as above shows that, if we replace
x
by
x^{\prime}
in (
8.3.2
) and
Claim
, they remain true. Write
y^{\prime}
for the projection of
x^{\prime}
to
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}},\mathbb{F}}
. Then again

\dim\overline{X}_{y^{\prime}}=\dim{T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x^{\prime}}=\dim{T}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},x}=\dim\mathscr{X}_{f_{\mathbf{I}_{\bm{A}}},\mathbb{F},\red}^{+},

where the equality in the middle follows, for example, from Corollary
6.4
. As a result, (
8.3.4
) holds
when we replace
y
by
y^{\prime}
. Let
x^{\prime}
run over
X^{\prime}(\mathbb{F})
, we see that (
8.3.4
) holds for Zariski dense
y^{\prime}\in Y(\mathbb{F})
. As a result, (
8.3.3
) is an equality.
\hfill\square
